73+50	To solve the problem of adding 73 and 50 in base-9, we need to follow the steps of addition while considering the base-9 system.\n\n1. **Convert the numbers to base-9:**\n   - The number 73 in base-10 needs to be converted to base-9. \n     - Divide 73 by 9: \(73 \div 9 = 8\) remainder \(1\). So, the least significant digit is 1.\n     - Divide 8 by 9: \(8 \div 9 = 0\) remainder \(8\). So, the next digit is 8.\n     - Therefore, 73 in base-10 is \(81_9\) in base-9.\n\n   - The number 50 in base-10 needs to be converted to base-9.\n     - Divide 50 by 9: \(50 \div 9 = 5\) remainder \(5\). So, the least significant digit is 5.\n     - Divide 5 by 9: \(5 \div 9 = 0\) remainder \(5\). So, the next digit is 5.\n     - Therefore, 50 in base-10 is \(55_9\) in base-9.\n\n2. **Add the numbers in base-9:**\n   - Align the numbers for addition:\n     \[\n     \begin{array}{c}\n       \phantom{+}81_9 \\\n     + 55_9 \\\n     \hline\n     \end{array}\n     \]\n\n   - Add the rightmost digits: \(1 + 5 = 6\). Since 6 is less than 9, we write 6 in the units place.\n\n   - Add the next digits: \(8 + 5 = 13\). Since 13 is greater than 9, we need to convert it:\n     - 13 in base-10 is \(1 \times 9 + 4\), so we write 4 and carry over 1 to the next column.\n\n   - Since there are no more digits to add, we add the carry: \(0 + 1 = 1\).\n\n   - Therefore, the sum is \(146_9\).\n\n3. **Final result:**\n   - The sum of 73 and 50 in base-9 is \(\boxed{146_9}\).

Instead, 9 is represented as \(10_9\) (1 in the next higher place value and 0 in the current place value). So, we write down 0 and carry over 1 to the next column.\n\n3. **Add the next column (left column)**: Now, add the digits in the next column (tens place), including any carry from the previous step.\n\n   - Left column: \(5 + 1 = 6\).\n   - Add the carry from the previous step: \(6 + 1 = 7\).\n\n   Since 7 is a valid digit in base-9, we write down 7.\n\n4. **Combine the results**: The result of the addition is obtained by combining the digits from each column.\n\n   - The left column gives us 7.\n   - The rightmost column gives us 0.\n\n   Therefore, the sum of 54 and 15 in base-9 is 70.\n\nThus, the final result of the addition \(54 + 15\) in base-9 is \(\boxed{70}\).

Find the largest power of 9 less than or equal to 88. The powers of 9 are \(9^0 = 1\), \(9^1 = 9\), \(9^2 = 81\), etc. Here, \(9^2 = 81\) is the largest power less than 88.\n2. 37+61	To solve the problem of adding 37 and 61 in base-9, we need to follow the steps of addition in base-9, similar to how we do in base-10, but keeping in mind that the digits range from 0 to 8.\n\n### Step 1: Convert the numbers to base-10\nFirst, let's convert the base-9 numbers to base-10 to understand their values:\n\n- The number 37 in base-9 is calculated as:\n  \[\n  3 \times 9^1 + 7 \times 9^0 = 3 \times 9 + 7 \times 1 = 27 + 7 = 34 \text{ in base-10}\n  \]\n\n- The number 61 in base-9 is calculated as:\n  \[\n  6 \times 9^1 + 1 \times 9^0 = 6 \times 9 + 1 \times 1 = 54 + 1 = 55 \text{ in base-10}\n  \]\n\n### Step 2: Add the base-10 equivalents\nNow, add the base-10 equivalents:\n\[\n34 + 55 = 89\n\]\n\n### Step 3: Convert the sum back to base-9\nNext, we convert the sum (89 in base-10) back to base-9:\n\n1. Find the largest power of 9 less than or equal to 89. The powers of 9 are \(9^0 = 1\), \(9^1 = 9\), \(9^2 = 81\), etc. Here, \(9^2 = 81\) is the largest power less than 89.\n\n2. Determine how many times 81 fits into 89:\n   \[\n   \left\lfloor \frac{89}{81} \\right\\rfloor = 1\n   \]\n   So, the coefficient for \(9^2\) is 1.\n\n3. Subtract \(81 \times 1 = 81\) from 89:\n   \[\n   89 - 81 = 8\n   \]\n\n4. Now, consider the next power, \(9^1 = 9\). Since 8 is less than 9, the coefficient for \(9^1\) is 0.\n\n5. Finally, the remaining 8 is the coefficient for \(9^0\).\n\nThus, the base-9 representation of 89 is \(108_9\).\n\n### Conclusion\nThe sum of 37 and 61 in base-9 is \(\boxed{108}\).

When a sum in a column reaches 9, it carries over to the next column, similar to how carrying works in base-10 when a sum reaches 10.\n\n### Step 2: Convert the Numbers to Base-9\nFirst, let's ensure that the numbers 30 and 75 are interpreted correctly in base-9.\n\n- The number 30 in base-9 is represented as \(3 \times 9^1 + 0 \times 9^0 = 27\) in base-10.\n- The number 75 in base-9 is represented as \(7 \times 9^1 + 5 \times 9^0 = 63 + 5 = 68\) in base-10.\n\n### Step 3: Add the Numbers in Base-9\nNow, let's add the numbers directly in base-9:\n\n```\n  30\n+ 75\n-----\n```\n\n#### Column by Column Addition\n1. **Rightmost Column (Units Place):**\n   - Add the digits: \(0 + 5 = 5\)\n   - Since 5 is less than 9, we write 5 in the units place.\n\n2. **Next Column (Nines Place):**\n   - Add the digits: \(3 + 7 = 10\)\n   - Since 10 is greater than 9, we need to carry over.\n   - 10 in base-9 is represented as \(1 \times 9^1 + 1 \times 9^0\), so we write 1 in the nines place and carry over 1 to the next column.\n\n3. **Carry Over:**\n   - Since there is no more column to add, the carry over becomes the leftmost digit.\n\n### Step 4: Write the Result\nThe result of the addition is \(105\) in base-9.\n\nThus, the sum of 30 and 75 in base-9 is \(\boxed{105}\).

In base-9, 12 is represented as \(13_9\) (since \(12 \div 9 = 1\) remainder 3). Write down 3 and carry over 1.\n     - Next, add the middle digits with the carry: \(0 + 5 + 1 = 6\). Write down 6.\n     - Finally, add the leftmost digits: \(1 + 0 = 1\). Write down 1.\n\n   - The result of the addition is \(163_9\).\n\n3. **Convert the result back to base-10 to verify:**\n   - \(163_9\) in base-9 is calculated as:\n     - \(1 \times 9^2 + 6 \times 9^1 + 3 \times 9^0 = 81 + 54 + 3 = 138\).\n\nThus, the sum of 50 and 88 in base-9 is \(\boxed{163_9}\).

88+50	To solve the problem of adding 88 and 50 in base-9, we need to first convert these numbers from base-10 to base-9, perform the addition in base-9, and then ensure the result is in base-9.\n\n### Step 1: Convert 88 and 50 from base-10 to base-9\n\n#### Convert 88 to base-9:\n1. Find the largest power of 9 less than or equal to 88. The powers of 9 are \(9^0 = 1\), \(9^1 = 9\), \(9^2 = 81\), \(9^3 = 729\), etc. Here, \(9^2 = 81\) is the largest power less than 88.\n2. Divide 88 by 81: \(88 \div 81 = 1\) with a remainder of \(88 - 81 = 7\).\n3. Next, divide the remainder 7 by \(9^1 = 9\): \(7 \div 9 = 0\) with a remainder of 7.\n4. Finally, divide the remainder 7 by \(9^0 = 1\): \(7 \div 1 = 7\) with no remainder.\n\nThus, 88 in base-9 is \(107_9\).\n\n#### Convert 50 to base-9:\n1. Find the largest power of 9 less than or equal to 50. Here, \(9^1 = 9\) is the largest power less than 50.\n2. Divide 50 by 9: \(50 \div 9 = 5\) with a remainder of \(50 - 45 = 5\).\n3. Finally, divide the remainder 5 by \(9^0 = 1\): \(5 \div 1 = 5\) with no remainder.\n\nThus, 50 in base-9 is \(55_9\).\n\n### Step 2: Add the numbers in base-9\n\nNow, add \(107_9\) and \(55_9\):\n\n```\n   107\n+   55\n------\n```\n\nStart from the rightmost digit:\n- Units place: \(7 + 5 = 12\). In base-9, 12 is represented as \(13_9\) (since \(12 \div 9 = 1\) remainder 3). Write down 3 and carry over 1.\n- Next, tens place: \(0 + 5 + 1 = 6\). Write down 6.\n- Finally, hundreds place: \(1 + 0 = 1\). Write down 1.\n\nThus, the sum is \(163_9\).\n\n### Conclusion\n\nThe result of adding 88 and 50 in base-9 is \(\boxed{163_9}\).
